\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}.\mathcal{B}}{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}}:\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{A}{a},\mathsf{rx}_{B}{b}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. Naturally, we have a homomorphism
\pi_{\mathcal{B}}\colon\mathcal{A}.\mathcal{B}\to\mathcal{A}
of reflexive graphs:

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\pi_{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\,\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle:\equiv x

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathcal{B}}^{\approx}\,\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p,q$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle:\equiv p

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{rx}}\,\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathbf{refl}

Definition 17

(Binary product of reflexive graphs)
.

If
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
are reflexive graphs, then we may form the product of reflexive graphs
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}
with vertices in
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\times\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
, where the type of edges
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle a_{0},b_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle a_{1},b_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is the cartesian product
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle a_{0}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}a_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\times\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle b_{0}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}}}b_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
with reflexivity data defined by
\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}}{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}}:\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x},\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{B}}{y}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

We can generalise
Definition

17
to arbitrary families.

Definition 18

(Product of reflexive graphs)
.

Let
A
be a type and let
\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
be a reflexive graph for each
x:A
. The product
\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
of the family of
\mathcal{B}
reflexive graphs is defined like so:

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\lvert}{\big\lvert}{\big\lvert}{\lvert}}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rvert}{\big\rvert}{\big\rvert}{\rvert}}}

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle f\mathrel{\approx_{\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}g

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{fx}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{gx}

\displaystyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{f}

\displaystyle:\equiv\lambda x\mathpunct{.}\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}

Definition 19

(Coproduct of reflexive graphs)
.

Let
A
be a type and let
\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
be a reflexive graph for each
x:A
. The coproduct
\mathchoice{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
of the family
\mathcal{B}
of reflexive graphs is defined like so:

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\lvert}{\big\lvert}{\big\lvert}{\lvert}}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathchoice{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rvert}{\big\rvert}{\big\rvert}{\rvert}}}

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle a_{0},b_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathchoice{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle a_{1},b_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p:a_{0}=_{A}a_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p:a_{0}=_{A}a_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p:a_{0}=_{A}a_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p:a_{0}=_{A}a_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}p_{*}^{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}b_{0}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle a_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}b_{1}

\displaystyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathchoice{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}}

\displaystyle:\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathbf{refl},\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle a$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{b}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

Later on in
Example

73
we will see how to refactor the construction of the coproduct of reflexive graphs in terms of a
lens
over the discrete reflexive graph
{\triangle}{A}
.

Definition 20

(Tensor and cotensor of reflexive graphs)
.

Let
A
be a type and let
\mathcal{B}
be a reflexive graph; then the
tensor
and
cotensor
of
\mathcal{B}
by
A
are defined respectively using coproducts and products as below:

\displaystyle A\cdot\mathcal{B}

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathchoice{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathcal{B}

(tensor)

\displaystyle A\pitchfork\mathcal{B}

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathcal{B}

(cotensor)

Definition 21

(Constant displayed reflexive graph)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
be reflexive graphs. The
constant displayed reflexive graph
over
\mathcal{A}
induced by
\mathcal{B}
is the displayed reflexive graph specified
\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{B}
below:

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle:\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{B}}^{p}}v

\displaystyle:\equiv u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}}}v

\displaystyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{B}}^{x}{u}

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{B}}{u}

Thus, evidently, we have
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}\equiv\mathcal{B}
for all
x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
.

Observation 22

(Binary product as a total reflexive graph)
.

For any two reflexive graphs
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
, we have
\mathcal{A}.\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\equiv\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}
.

Definition 23

(Subgraph comprehension)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a reflexive graph, and let
P\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
be a proposition for each vertex
x:\mathcal{A}
. We define the
comprehension
of
P
to be the following reflexive graph:

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:\mathcal{A}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\SavedStyle P\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}P\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,p$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mathrel{\approx_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:\mathcal{A}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\SavedStyle P\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y,q$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle:\equiv x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y

\displaystyle\mathsf{rx}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:\mathcal{A}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\SavedStyle P\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,p$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}

Construction 24

(Restriction of iterated displayed reflexive graphs)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a reflexive graph object, and let
\mathcal{B}
be a displayed reflexive graph over
\mathcal{A}
, and let
\mathcal{C}
be a displayed reflexive graph over
\mathcal{A}.\mathcal{B}
. Then for any
x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
, we may define a displayed reflexive graph
\mathcal{C}_{|\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}
over the component
\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
with vertices given as follows:

u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{C}_{|\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle u$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{C}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,u$}\mathclose{}{)}}

Displayed edges and reflexivity data are defined in terms of those of
\mathcal{C}
like so:

u,v:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}};p:u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}v;c:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{C}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,u$}\mathclose{}{)}},d:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{C}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,v$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\vdash c\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{C}_{|\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}^{p}}d:\equiv c\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x},p$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}d

u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}},c:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{C}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,u$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\vdash\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{C}_{|\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}^{u}{c}:\equiv\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x},u$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{c}

Computation 25

(Components of the restriction)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a reflexive graph, and let
\mathcal{B}
be a displayed reflexive graph over
\mathcal{A}
, and let
\mathcal{C}
be a displayed reflexive graph over
\mathcal{A}.\mathcal{B}
. Given
x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
and
u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
, the component
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{C}_{|\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle u$}\mathclose{}{)}}
of the restriction of
\mathcal{C}
to
\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
at
u
is definitionally equal to the component
\mathcal{C}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,u$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
of
\mathcal{C}
at
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,u$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}.\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
.

2.2
Duality involution for reflexive graphs

Definition 26

(Opposite reflexive graph)
.

We define the
opposite
of a reflexive graph
\mathcal{A}
as follows:

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{op}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle:\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{op}}}}y

\displaystyle:\equiv y\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}x

\displaystyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{op}}}{x}

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}

Observation 27
.

The opposite reflexive graph operation is definitionally involutive: we have
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{op}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\mathsf{op}}\equiv\mathcal{A}
definitionally.

Definition 28

(Total opposite of a displayed reflexive graph)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a reflexive graph, and let
\mathcal{B}
be a displayed reflexive graph over
\mathcal{A}
. We define the
total opposite

\mathcal{B}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}
of
\mathcal{B}
to be the following displayed reflexive graph over
\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{op}}
,

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle:\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}}^{p}}v

\displaystyle:\equiv v\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}}u

\displaystyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{B}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}}^{x}{u}

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{B}}^{x}{u}\text{,}

so that we have
(\mathcal{A}.\mathcal{B})^{\mathsf{op}}\equiv\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{op}}.\mathcal{B}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}
.

Note that
Definition

28
does not define the actual “opposite” of a displayed reflexive graph
\mathcal{B}
, which would naturally have the same base as
\mathcal{B}
; opposites of arbitrary displayed reflexive graphs do not make sense (for the same reason that Bénabou’s definition of opposites applies only to displayed categories that are additionally fibrations).

Observation

27
extends to the following duality involution on displayed reflexive graphs vs
Definition

28
.

Observation 29

(Duality involution for displayed reflexive graphs)
.

The operation sending a displayed reflexive graph to its total opposite is definitionally involutive,
i.e
.
we have
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}\equiv\mathcal{B}
.

2.3
Path objects and the univalence condition

Definition 30

(Fans of a vertex)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a reflexive graph. The
fan
of a vertex
x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
is defined to be the type
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}:\equiv\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y
of vertices equipped with an edge from
x
; dually, the
co-fan
of a vertex
x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
is defined to be the type
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{-}:\equiv\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}y\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}x
of vertices equipped with an edge
toward

x
.

Lemma 31
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a reflexive graph; then every fan of
\mathcal{A}
is a proposition if and only if every co-fan of
\mathcal{A}
is a proposition.

Proof.

First we assume that every fan is contractible:

x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}};u_{0},u_{1}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{-}\vdash u_{0}=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{-}}u_{1}

by reassociating

x,y_{0},y_{1}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}};p_{0}:y_{0}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}x,p_{1}:y_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}x\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y_{0},p_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{-}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y_{1},p_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y_{0}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}
is contractible by assumption

y_{0},y_{1}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}};p_{1}:y_{1}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y_{0}\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y_{0},\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{y_{0}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y_{0}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{-}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y_{1},p_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y_{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}
is contractible by assumption

y_{1}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y_{1},\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{y_{1}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y_{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{-}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y_{1},\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{y_{1}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

by reflexivity

Conversely, assume that every co-fan is contractible.

x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}};u_{0},u_{1}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}\vdash u_{0}=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}}u_{1}

by reassociating

x,y_{0},y_{1}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}};p_{0}:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y_{0},p_{1}:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y_{1}\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y_{0},p_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y_{1},p_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y_{0}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{-}
is contractible by assumption

y_{0},y_{1}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}};p_{1}:y_{0}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y_{1}\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y_{0},\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{y_{0}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y_{0}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y_{1},p_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y_{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{-}
is contractible by assumption

y_{1}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y_{1},\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{y_{1}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y_{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y_{1},\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{y_{1}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

by reflexivity

∎

Construction 32

(From identifications to edges)
.

The reflexivity datum of a reflexive graph
\mathcal{A}
induces a function from identifications to edges as follows:

x,y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}};p:x=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}y\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lfloor}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rfloor}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y

by identification induction

x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lfloor}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathbf{refl}$}\mathclose{}{\rfloor}}^{x,x}_{\mathcal{A}}:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}x

by reflexivity

x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lfloor}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathbf{refl}$}\mathclose{}{\rfloor}}^{x,x}_{\mathcal{A}}:\equiv\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}

When it causes no ambiguity, we will write
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lfloor}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rfloor}}_{\mathcal{A}}
for
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lfloor}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rfloor}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}
.

Lemma 33

(From edges to identifications via propositional fans)
.

Suppose that each fan
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}
of a reflexive graph
\mathcal{A}
is a proposition. Then each
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lfloor}\hbox{$\SavedStyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rfloor}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}
has a quasi-inverse
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}\colon x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y\to x=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}y
and is therefore an equivalence.

Proof.

Let
\Phi_{x}
be the proof that a given fan
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}
is a proposition. Rather than defining
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y\to x=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}y
directly, we construct a slightly more general function that anticipates the contractibility of
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}
.

x,y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}};p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y;\phi:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y,p$}\mathclose{}{)}}\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p;\phi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}:x=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}y

by identification induction

x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x};\mathbf{refl}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}:x=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}x

by reflexivity

x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x};\mathbf{refl}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}:\equiv\mathbf{refl}

Then we define
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}
by instantiation.

x,y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}};p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}:\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p;\Phi_{x}\,\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y,p$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}

Next we prove that
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}
is a section of
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lfloor}\hbox{$\SavedStyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rfloor}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}
.

x,y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}};p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lfloor}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}$}\mathclose{}{\rfloor}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}=_{x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y}p

by definition

\ldots\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lfloor}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p;\Phi_{x}\,\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y,p$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}$}\mathclose{}{\rfloor}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}=_{x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y}p

by generalising over
\Phi_{x}\,\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y,p$}\mathclose{}{)}}

x,y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}};p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y,\phi:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y,p$}\mathclose{}{)}}\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lfloor}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p;\phi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}$}\mathclose{}{\rfloor}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}=_{x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y}p

by identification induction

x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lfloor}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x};\mathbf{refl}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{x,x}_{\mathcal{A}}$}\mathclose{}{\rfloor}}^{x,x}_{\mathcal{A}}=_{x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}x}\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}

by definition of
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle-;-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{x,x}_{\mathcal{A}}

x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lfloor}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathbf{refl}$}\mathclose{}{\rfloor}}^{x,x}_{\mathcal{A}}=_{x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}x}\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}

by definition of
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lfloor}\hbox{$\SavedStyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rfloor}}^{x,x}_{\mathcal{A}}

x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\vdash\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}=_{x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}x}\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}

by reflexivity

Finally, we prove that
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}
is a retraction of
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lfloor}\hbox{$\SavedStyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rfloor}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}
.

x,y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}};p:x=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}y\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lfloor}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rfloor}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}=_{x=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}y}p

by identification induction

x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lfloor}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathbf{refl}$}\mathclose{}{\rfloor}}^{x,x}_{\mathcal{A}}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{x,x}_{\mathcal{A}}=_{x=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}x}\mathbf{refl}

by definition

x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}x;\Phi_{x}\,(x,\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}x)\,(x,\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}x)$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{x,x}_{\mathcal{A}}=_{x=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}x}\mathbf{refl}

because
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}
is a set

x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}x;\mathbf{refl}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{x,x}_{\mathcal{A}}=_{x=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}x}\mathbf{refl}

by definition

x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\vdash\mathbf{refl}=_{x=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}x}\mathbf{refl}

by reflexivity

∎

Lemma 34
.

For a reflexive graph
\mathcal{A}
, each function
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lfloor}\hbox{$\SavedStyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rfloor}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}
is an equivalence if and only if each fan
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle z$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}
is a proposition.

Proof.

We have seen one direction already in
Lemma

33
. Conversely,
suppose that each component of
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lfloor}\hbox{$\SavedStyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rfloor}}^{x,y}_{\mathcal{A}}
is an equivalence; then clearly each
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle z$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}\equiv\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}z\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y
is a retract of
\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}x=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}y\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}
, which is contractible by the contractibility of singletons. Therefore, each fan is contractible and hence a proposition.
∎

Definition 35

(Univalent reflexive graph)
.

A reflexive graph
\mathcal{A}
is called
univalent
when any of the following equivalent conditions hold:

1.

Each fan
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}
is a proposition.

2.

Each co-fan
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{-}
is a proposition.

3.

Each fan
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}
is contractible with centre
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

4.

Each co-fan
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{-}
is contractible with centre
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

5.

Each of the functions
\mathsf{idToEdge}_{x,y}:x=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}y\to x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y
defined in
Construction

32
is an equivalence.

We shall refer to a univalent reflexive graph as a
path object
.

Proof.

The equivalence of the stated conditions follows from
Lemmas

31
and
34
.
∎

Definition 36

(Univalent displayed reflexive graph)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a reflexive graph. A displayed reflexive graph
\mathcal{B}
over
\mathcal{A}
is called
univalent
when for each
x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
, the component (
Definition

12
)
\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is univalent in the sense of
Definition

35
.
We shall refer to a univalent displayed reflexive graph as a
displayed path object
, regardless of whether the base
\mathcal{A}
is univalent.

2.4
Path algebra in a path object

Edges in an arbitrary reflexive graph cannot be concatenated or inverted; when the reflexive graph is
univalent
,
i.e
.
a
path object
, the type of edges becomes (by
Lemma

34
) equivalent to the first level of the canonical
\infty
-groupoid structure on the type of vertices, and as such brooks fully coherent concatenation and inversion operations.
We will need these operations only rarely — and indeed, the central thesis of the theory of path objects in univalent foundations is that it is often possible to avoid painful path algebra by very careful choices of path object structure.

Construction 37

(Path algebra toolkit)
.

Given any path object
\mathcal{A}
, we may construct the following terms facilitating path algebra in
\mathcal{A}
.

p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y,q:y\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}z\vdash p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}{\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\centerdot$}}{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\,\centerdot\,$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\,\centerdot\,$}}}_{\mathcal{A}}q:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}z

p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y\vdash p^{-1}_{\mathcal{A}}:y\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}x

p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y

\vdash\mathsf{runit}_{p}:p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}{\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\centerdot$}}{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\,\centerdot\,$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\,\centerdot\,$}}}_{\mathcal{A}}\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{y}=_{x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y}p

\vdash\mathsf{lunit}_{p}:\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}{\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\centerdot$}}{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\,\centerdot\,$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\,\centerdot\,$}}}_{\mathcal{A}}p=_{x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y}p

\vdash\mathsf{rsym}_{p}:p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}{\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\centerdot$}}{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\,\centerdot\,$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\,\centerdot\,$}}}_{\mathcal{A}}p^{-1}_{\mathcal{A}}=_{x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}x}\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}

\vdash\mathsf{lsym}_{p}:p^{-1}_{\mathcal{A}}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}{\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\centerdot$}}{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\,\centerdot\,$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\,\centerdot\,$}}}_{\mathcal{A}}p=_{y\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y}\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{y}

p:u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}v,q:v\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}w,r:w\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}x

\vdash\mathsf{assoc}_{p,q,r}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}{\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\centerdot$}}{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\,\centerdot\,$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\,\centerdot\,$}}}_{\mathcal{A}}q$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}{\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\centerdot$}}{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\,\centerdot\,$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\,\centerdot\,$}}}_{\mathcal{A}}r=_{u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}x}p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}{\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\centerdot$}}{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\,\centerdot\,$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\,\centerdot\,$}}}_{\mathcal{A}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle q\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}{\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\centerdot$}}{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\,\centerdot\,$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\,\centerdot\,$}}}_{\mathcal{A}}r$}\mathclose{}{)}}

Of course, there is no end to the possible combinators, so we have presented just a few representative examples that we will make use of.

Lemma 38

(Pre-concatenation equivalence)
.

For a path object
\mathcal{A}
and an edge
p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y
, we have a family of equivalences induced by pre-concatenation with
p
as below,

z:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}{\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\centerdot$}}{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\,\centerdot\,$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\,\centerdot\,$}}}_{\mathcal{A}}-$}\mathclose{}{)}}\colon y\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}z\to x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}z

with chosen inverse
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p^{-1}_{\mathcal{A}}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\displaystyle\centerdot$}}{\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\centerdot$}}{\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\,\centerdot\,$}}{\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\,\centerdot\,$}}}_{\mathcal{A}}-$}\mathclose{}{)}}:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}z\to y\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}z
.

Proof.

Simple path algebra using
\mathsf{assoc}
,
\mathsf{lunit}
,
\mathsf{lsym}
, and
\mathsf{rsym}
from
Construction

37
.
∎

2.5
Univalent families and reflexive graph images

Definition 39

(Reflexive graph image)
.

The
reflexive graph image
of a type
A
under a family of types
x:A\vdash B\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\mathbf{type}
is defined to be the following reflexive graph
A/B
:

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A/B$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle:\equiv A

\displaystyle x\mathrel{\approx_{A/B}}y

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathsf{Equiv}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle B\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}},B\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{rx}_{A/B}{x}

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathsf{idnEquiv}_{B\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}

Definition 40

(Univalent family of types)
.

Let
A
be a type, and let
B
be a family of types indexed in
A
. The pair
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is said to be
univalent
when any of the following equivalent conditions hold:

1.

For each
x:A
, the type
\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathsf{Equiv}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle B\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}},B\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is a proposition.

2.

For each
x:A
, the type
\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathsf{Equiv}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle B\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}},B\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is a contractible.

3.

For each
x,y:A
the canonical map
x=_{A}y\to\mathsf{Equiv}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle B\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}},B\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
sending
\mathbf{refl}
to the identity equivalence is an equivalence.

4.

The reflexive graph image
A/B
is univalent.

Proof.

For (1,2), inhabited types are propositions if and only if they are contractible. The remainder is the “fundamental theorem of identity types”
(
Rijke, 2025
)
.
∎

One often refers to a univalent family of types
(U,E)
as a
universe
to emphasise that it may be closed under various type constructors,
etc
.

Definition 41

(Propositional reflexive graph image)
.

Let
B\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
be a proposition for each
x:A
. The
propositional reflexive graph image
of
A
under
B
is defined to be the following reflexive graph
A/_{-1}B
:

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A/_{-1}B$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle:\equiv A

\displaystyle x\mathrel{\approx_{A/_{-1}B}}y

\displaystyle:\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle B\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to B\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\times\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle B\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to B\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{rx}_{A/_{-1}B}{x}

\displaystyle:\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\lambda{u}\mathpunct{.}{u},\lambda{u}\mathpunct{.}{u}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

Observation 42
.

Let
B
be a family of propositions indexed in
A
. The pair
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is univalent if and only if the propositional reflexive graph image
A/_{-1}B
is univalent.

Definition 43

(Univalent family of path objects)
.

A
univalent family of path objects
is defined to be a pair
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle U,\mathcal{E}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
where
U
is a type and
A:U\vdash\mathcal{E}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is a family of path objects in
U
such that the reflexive graph image
U/\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{E}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
is univalent.

2.6
Basic closure properties of path objects

Lemma 44

(Opposite path object)
.

A reflexive graph
\mathcal{A}
is univalent if and only if its opposite
\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{op}}
is univalent.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of
Lemma

31
.
∎

Lemma 45

(Total path object)
.

If
\mathcal{A}
is a path object and
\mathcal{B}
is a displayed path object over
\mathcal{A}
, then
\mathcal{A}.\mathcal{B}
is a path object.

Proof.

Univalence is established as follows.

u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}.\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}};s_{0},s_{1}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle u$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}.\mathcal{B}}^{+}\vdash s_{0}=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle u$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}.\mathcal{B}}^{+}}s_{1}

by reassociating and unfolding hypotheses

x,x_{0},x_{1}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}};y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},y_{0}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},y_{1}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},

p_{0}:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}x_{0},p_{1}:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}x_{1},q_{0}:y\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}}^{p_{0}}}y_{0},q_{1}:y\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}}^{p_{1}}}y_{1}

\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x_{0},y_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p_{0},q_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}.\mathcal{B}}^{+}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x_{1},y_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p_{1},q_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

as
\mathcal{A}
is a path object and thus
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}
is contractible

x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},y_{0}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},y_{1}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},

q_{0}:y\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}y_{0},q_{1}:y\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}y_{1}

\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,y_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x},q_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}.\mathcal{B}}^{+}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,y_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x},q_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

as
\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is a path object and thus
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}^{+}
is contractible

x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x},\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{y}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}.\mathcal{B}}^{+}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x},\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{y}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

by reflexivity

∎

Observation 46

(Constant displayed path object)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a reflexive graph and let
\mathcal{B}
be a path object. Then the constant displayed reflexive graph
\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{B}
over
\mathcal{A}
is univalent.

Proof.

This follows immediately, considering that we have
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}\equiv\mathcal{B}
for each
x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
by definition.
∎

Corollary 47

(Binary product of path objects)
.

If
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
are path objects, then so is
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}
.

Proof.

By
22
and
46
.
∎

Definition 48

(Function extensionality)
.

Dependent function extensionality
is precisely the property that for each type
A
and family
\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
of path objects indexed in
x:A
, the product
\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is a path object. Likewise,
non-dependent function extensionality
states that for each type
A
and path object
\mathcal{B}
, the cotensor
A\pitchfork\mathcal{B}
is a path object.

Lemma 49

(Coproduct of path objects)
.

For any type
A
and family
\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
of path objects indexed in
x:A
, the coproduct
\mathchoice{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is a path object.

Proof.

We establish the univalence condition as follows.

u:\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}};s_{0},s_{1}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle u$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathchoice{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}^{+}\vdash s_{0}=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle u$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathchoice{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}^{+}}s_{1}

by reassociating and unfolding hypotheses

x,x_{0},x_{1}:A;y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},y_{0}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},y_{1}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},p_{0}:x=_{A}x_{0},p_{1}:x=_{A}x_{1},

q_{0}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{*}^{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}y\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}y_{0},q_{1}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{*}{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}y\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}y_{1}

\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x_{0},y_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p_{0},q_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathchoice{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}^{+}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x_{1},y_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p_{1},q_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

by based identification induction on
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x_{0},p_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

x,x_{1}:A;y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},y_{0}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},y_{1}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},p_{1}:x=_{A}x_{1},

q_{0}:y\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}y_{0},q_{1}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{*}{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle-$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}y\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}y_{1}

\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,y_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathbf{refl},q_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathchoice{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}^{+}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x_{1},y_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p_{1},q_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

by based identification induction on
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x_{1},p_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

x:A;y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},y_{0}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},y_{1}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},q_{0}:y\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}y_{0},q_{1}:y\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}y_{1}

\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,y_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathbf{refl},q_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathchoice{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}^{+}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,y_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathbf{refl},q_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

as
\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is a path object and thus
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}^{+}
is contractible

x:A;y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathbf{refl},\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{y}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=_{\ldots}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathbf{refl},\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{y}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

by reflexivity

∎

Corollary 50

(Tensor of path objects)
.

The tensor
A\cdot\mathcal{B}
of a path object
\mathcal{B}
by a type
A
is a path object.

Corollary 51

(Discrete path object)
.

The discrete reflexive graph
{\triangle}{A}
on any type
A
is univalent.

Proof.

This is precisely the contractibility of singletons.
∎

Lemma 52

(Codiscrete path object)
.

The codiscrete reflexive graph
{\triangledown}{A}
on a type
A
is univalent if and only if
A
is a proposition.

Proof.

Suppose that
\mathcal{A}
is a proposition. Then univalence is established like so:

x:A;s_{0},s_{1}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{{\triangledown}{A}}^{+}\vdash s_{0}=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{{\triangledown}{A}}^{+}}s_{1}

by reassociating and unfolding hypotheses

x,x_{0},x_{1}:A\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x_{0},*$}\mathclose{}{)}}=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{{\triangledown}{A}}^{+}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x_{1},*$}\mathclose{}{)}}

as
A
is a proposition

x:A\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,*$}\mathclose{}{)}}=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{{\triangledown}{A}}^{+}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,*$}\mathclose{}{)}}

by reflexivity

∎

Conversely, assume that
{\triangledown}{A}
is univalent. For any two
x,y:A
,
we evidently have
*:x\mathrel{\approx_{{\triangledown}{A}}}y
and thus
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle*$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{x,y}_{{\triangledown}{A}}:x=_{A}y
by
Lemma

33
.

Lemma 53

(Path subobject comprehension)
.

If
\mathcal{A}
is a path object and
P\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is a proposition for each vertex
x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
, then the comprehension
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:\mathcal{A}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\SavedStyle P\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
is a path object.

Proof.

Univalence is established as follows:

u:\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}P\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}};s_{0},s_{1}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle u$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:\mathcal{A}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\SavedStyle P\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}}^{+}\vdash s_{0}=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle u$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:\mathcal{A}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\SavedStyle P\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}}^{+}}s_{1}

by reassociating and unfolding hypotheses

x,x_{0},x_{1}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}};y:P\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}},y_{0}:P\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}},y_{1}:P\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}},p_{0}:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}x_{0},p_{1}:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}x_{1}

\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x_{0},y_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p_{0},*$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:\mathcal{A}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\SavedStyle P\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}}^{+}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x_{1},y_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p_{1},*$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

as
\mathcal{A}
is a path object and so
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}
is contractible

x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}};y,y_{0},y_{1}:P\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,y_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x},*$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=_{\ldots}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,y_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x},*$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

as
P\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is a proposition

x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},y:P\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x},*$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=_{\ldots}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x},*$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

by reflexivity

∎
