3
Lenses of reflexive graphs and path objects

In this section, we are concerned with various kinds of
families
of ordinary reflexive graphs, and how they give rise to certain naturally occurring
displayed
reflexive graphs.

3.1
Lenses of reflexive graphs

We introduce two dual kinds of lens of reflexive graphs.

Definition 54

(Oplax covariant lens)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a reflexive graph. An
oplax covariant lens
of reflexive graphs over
\mathcal{A}
is defined to be a family of reflexive graphs
\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
indexed in
x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
equipped with the following data:

\displaystyle x,y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}};p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y,u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{push}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{pushRx}_{\mathcal{B}}^{x}u:\mathsf{push}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}}{u}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}u

Definition 55

(Lax contravariant lens)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a reflexive graph. A
lax contravariant lens
of reflexive graphs over
\mathcal{A}
is defined to be a family of reflexive graphs
\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
indexed in
x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
equipped with the following data:

\displaystyle x,y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}};p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y,u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{pull}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{pullRx}_{\mathcal{B}}^{x}u:u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathsf{pull}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}}{u}

In the context of
Definition

54
or
Definition

55
, we will refer to
\mathcal{B}
as a
lens of path objects
or a
univalent lens
when each
\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is univalent.

Definition 56

(Display of oplax covariant lenses)
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be an oplax covariant lens over a reflexive graph
\mathcal{A}
. The (covariant)
display
of
\mathcal{B}
over
\mathcal{A}
is defined to be the following displayed reflexive graph
\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{B}
over
\mathcal{A}
:

\displaystyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y,u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},v:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}}^{p}}v:\equiv\mathsf{push}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}v

\displaystyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{rx}_{\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}}^{x}{u}:\equiv\mathsf{pushRx}_{\mathcal{B}}^{x}{u}

Definition 57

(Display of lax contravariant lenses)
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a lax contravariant lens over a reflexive graph
\mathcal{A}
. The (contravariant)
display
of
\mathcal{B}
over
\mathcal{A}
is defined to be the following displayed reflexive graph
\mathsf{disp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}
over
\mathcal{A}
:

\displaystyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y,u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},v:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{disp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}}^{p}}v:\equiv u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathsf{pull}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}{v}

\displaystyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{rx}_{\mathsf{disp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}}^{x}{u}:\equiv\mathsf{pullRx}_{\mathcal{B}}^{x}{u}

Computation 58

(Components of the display of oplax covariant lenses)
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be an oplax covariant lens over a reflexive graph
\mathcal{A}
. For any
x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
, the component
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is precisely the following:

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle u\mathrel{\approx_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}v

\displaystyle\equiv\mathsf{push}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}}{u}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}v

\displaystyle\mathsf{rx}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{u}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathsf{pushRx}_{\mathcal{B}}^{x}{u}

We likewise have the following description of fans:

\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle u$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}^{+}\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{push}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}}{u}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}^{+}

(Components of the display of lax contravariant lenses)
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a lax contravariant lens over a reflexive graph
\mathcal{A}
. For any
x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
, the component
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is unfolds as follows:

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle u\mathrel{\approx_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}v

\displaystyle\equiv u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathsf{pull}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}}{v}

\displaystyle\mathsf{rx}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{u}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathsf{pullRx}_{\mathcal{B}}^{x}{u}

We have the following description of co-fans:

\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle u$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}^{-}\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{pull}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}}{u}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}^{-}

Lemma 60

(Display of oplax covariant lenses of path objects)
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be an oplax covariant lens over a reflexive graph
\mathcal{A}
. If each
\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is univalent, then the display
\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}
is univalent.

Proof.

We must check that each component
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is univalent. It suffices to check that for each
u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
, the fan
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle u$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}^{+}
is a proposition. By
Computation

58
, we have
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle u$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}^{+}\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{push}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}}{u}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}^{+}
, which is a proposition by our assumption that
\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is univalent.
∎

Lemma 61

(Display of lax contravariant lenses of path objects)
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a lax contravariant lens over a reflexive graph
\mathcal{A}
. If each
\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is univalent, then the display
\mathsf{disp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}
is univalent.

Proof.

Analogous to
Lemma

60
.
∎

3.2
Unbiased dependent lenses

In this section, we describe a slightly strange technical device that generalises both oplax covariant and lax contravariant lenses that we name the
unbiased dependent lens
. The reason for introducing these is to characterise the identity types of structures involving mixed variance (including, for example, the type of reflexive graphs!).

Definition 62

(Unbiased dependent lens)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a reflexive graph. A
unbiased dependent lens
over
\mathcal{A}
is defined to be a family of reflexive graphs
\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p$}\mathclose{}{)}}
indexed in edges
p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y
equipped with the following data:

\displaystyle x,y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}};p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y,u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{lext}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}{u}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle x,y:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}};p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y,u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{y}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{rext}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}{u}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{extRx}_{\mathcal{B}}^{x}{u}:\mathsf{lext}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}}{u}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathsf{rext}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}}{u}

\displaystyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{rextRx}_{\mathcal{B}}^{x}{u}:u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathsf{rext}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}}{u}

Definition 63

(Display of unbiased dependent lenses)
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a unbiased dependent lens over a reflexive graph
\mathcal{A}
. The
display
of
\mathcal{B}
over
\mathcal{A}
is defined to be the following displayed reflexive graph
\mathsf{disp}^{\pm}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}
over
\mathcal{A}
:

\displaystyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{\pm}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y,u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},v:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{disp}^{\pm}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}}^{p}}v:\equiv\mathsf{lext}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}{u}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathsf{rext}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}{u}

\displaystyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{rx}_{\mathsf{disp}^{\pm}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}}^{x}{u}:\equiv\mathsf{extRx}_{\mathcal{B}}^{x}{u}

Remark 64
.

Only
\mathsf{extRx}_{\mathcal{B}}^{x}{u}
was needed to define the display of unbiased dependent lenses, whereas the lax unitor
u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathsf{rext}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}}{u}
played no role. The reason for including the latter is that it is required in order for univalence of each
\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p$}\mathclose{}{)}}
to imply univalence for the display
\mathsf{disp}^{\pm}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}
.
Naturally, univalence would
also
follow if we instead included an oplax unitor
\mathsf{lext}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}{u}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}u
. What we must not do is include both
\mathsf{lext}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}{u}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}u
and
u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathsf{rext}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}}{u}
, as we intend the type of
fiberwise univalent
unbiased dependent lens structures on a family of path objects over a path object to be a proposition. This situation is somewhat similar to that of
half-adjoint equivalences
in homotopy type theory being coherent only by virtue of omitting one of the snake identities which can be obtained from the other. Of course, our current situation in the world of reflexive graphs is a bit different as we cannot obtain the lax unitor from the oplax unitor, and vice versa: as these unitors play no computational role, however, we will let it slide.

Lemma 65

(Display of unbiased dependent lenses of path objects)
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a unbiased dependent lens over a reflexive graph
\mathcal{A}
. If each
\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is univalent, then so is the display
\mathsf{disp}^{\pm}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}
.

The following
Construction

66
shows that unbiased dependent lenses generalise
both
oplax covariant and lax contravariant lenses up to definitional equality.

Construction 66

(From biased to unbiased lenses)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a path object.

1.

Any oplax covariant lens
\mathcal{B}
over
\mathcal{A}
induces a unbiased dependent lens
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{+}^{\pm}
over
\mathcal{A}
such that
\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}\equiv\mathsf{disp}^{\pm}_{\mathcal{A}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{+}^{\pm}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
definitionally:

\displaystyle p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y

\displaystyle\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{+}^{\pm}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\equiv\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y,u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{lext}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{+}^{\pm}}^{p}u:\equiv\mathsf{push}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}{u}

\displaystyle p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y,u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{rext}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{+}^{\pm}}^{p}u:\equiv u

\displaystyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{extRx}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{+}^{\pm}}^{x}{u}:\equiv\mathsf{pushRx}_{\mathcal{B}}^{x}{u}

\displaystyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{rextRx}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{+}^{\pm}}^{x}{u}:\equiv\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{u}

2.

Any lax contravariant lens
\mathcal{B}
over
\mathcal{A}
induces a unbiased dependent lens
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{-}^{\pm}
over
\mathcal{A}
such that
\mathsf{disp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}\equiv\mathsf{disp}^{\pm}_{\mathcal{A}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{-}^{\pm}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
definitionally:

\displaystyle p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y

\displaystyle\vdash\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{-}^{\pm}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\equiv\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y,u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{lext}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{-}^{\pm}}^{p}u:\equiv u

\displaystyle p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y,u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{rext}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{-}^{\pm}}^{p}{u}:\equiv\mathsf{pull}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}{u}

\displaystyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{extRx}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{-}^{\pm}}^{x}{u}:\equiv\mathsf{pullRx}_{\mathcal{B}}^{x}{u}

\displaystyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{rextRx}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{-}^{\pm}}^{x}{u}:\equiv\mathsf{pullRx}_{\mathcal{B}}^{x}{u}

Computation.

We compute the displays as follows.

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{\pm}_{\mathcal{A}}{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{+}^{\pm}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{+}^{\pm}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{\pm}_{\mathcal{A}}{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{-}^{\pm}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{-}^{\pm}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{disp}^{\pm}_{\mathcal{A}}{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{+}^{\pm}}}^{p}}v\equiv\mathsf{lext}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{+}^{\pm}}^{p}{u}\mathrel{\approx_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{+}^{\pm}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathsf{rext}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{+}^{\pm}}^{p}{v}\equiv\mathsf{push}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}{u}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}u\equiv u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}}^{p}}v

\displaystyle u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{disp}^{\pm}_{\mathcal{A}}{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{-}^{\pm}}}^{p}}v\equiv\mathsf{lext}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{-}^{\pm}}^{p}{u}\mathrel{\approx_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{-}^{\pm}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathsf{rext}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{-}^{\pm}}^{p}{v}\equiv u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathsf{pull}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}{v}\equiv u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{disp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}}^{p}}v

\displaystyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathsf{disp}^{\pm}_{\mathcal{A}}{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{+}^{\pm}}}^{x}{u}\equiv\mathsf{extRx}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{+}^{\pm}}^{x}{u}\equiv\mathsf{push}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}{u}\equiv\mathsf{rx}_{\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}}^{x}{u}

\displaystyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathsf{disp}^{\pm}_{\mathcal{A}}{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{-}^{\pm}}}^{x}{u}\equiv\mathsf{extRx}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{-}^{\pm}}^{x}{u}\equiv\mathsf{pull}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}{u}\equiv\mathsf{rx}_{\mathsf{disp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}}^{x}{u}

3.3
Duality involution for reflexive graph lenses

We have seen in
Definitions

26
and
27
the duality involution for reflexive graphs that flips the direction of edges. This involution hosts a (definitional) equivalence between the types of oplax covariant and lax contravariant lenses respectively. By virtue of this equivalence, from a result about all oplax covariant lenses one obtains automatically a dual result about all lax contravariant lenses.

Definition 67

(Total opposite of a lens)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a reflexive graph, and let
\mathcal{B}
be an oplax covariant lens of reflexive graphs over
\mathcal{A}
. We define the
total opposite
of
\mathcal{B}
to be the following lax contravariant lens
\mathcal{B}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}
over
\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{op}}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\mathsf{op}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{pull}_{\mathcal{B}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}}^{p}{u}

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathsf{push}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}{u}

\displaystyle\mathsf{pullRx}_{\mathcal{B}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}}^{x}{u}

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathsf{pushRx}_{\mathcal{B}}^{x}{u}

Conversely, if
\mathcal{B}
is a lax contravariant lens we define its total opposite to be the following lax contravariant lens
\mathcal{B}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}
over
\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{op}}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\mathsf{op}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{push}_{\mathcal{B}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}}^{p}{u}

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathsf{pull}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}{u}

\displaystyle\mathsf{pushRx}_{\mathcal{B}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}}^{x}{u}

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathsf{pullRx}_{\mathcal{B}}^{x}{u}

The purpose of
total
opposites is to exhibit a duality involution identifying oplax covariant and lax contravariant lenses.

Observation 68

(Duality involution for lenses)
.

By virtue of
Definition

67
, we see that an oplax covariant lens over
\mathcal{A}
is precisely the same thing as a lax contravariant lens over
\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{op}}
; and, conversely, that a lax contravariant lens over
\mathcal{A}
is precisely the same thing as an oplax covariant lens over
\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{op}}
.

Lemma 69

(Display of total opposites)
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be an oplax covariant lens of reflexive graphs over a reflexive graph
\mathcal{A}
. Then we have a definitional equivalence
\mathsf{disp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{op}}}{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}
of displayed reflexive graphs.

Proof.

We compute explicitly.

\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{op}}}{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}

by
Definition

57

{}\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

by
Definition

67

{}\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\mathsf{op}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

by
Definition

26

{}\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}

by
Definition

28

{}\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}

by
Definition

56

{}\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{disp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{op}}}{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}^{p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{op}}}}y}}v

by
Definition

57

{}\equiv u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathsf{pull}_{\mathcal{B}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}}^{p}{v}

by
Definition

67

{}\equiv u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\mathsf{op}}}}\mathsf{push}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}{v}

by
Definition

26

{}\equiv\mathsf{push}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}{v}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}u

u\mathrel{\approx_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}}^{p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{op}}}}y}}v

by
Definition

28

{}\equiv v\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}}^{p:y\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}x}}v

by
Definition

56

{}\equiv\mathsf{push}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}{v}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}u

\mathsf{rx}_{\mathsf{disp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{op}}}{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}^{x}{u}

by
Definition

57

{}\equiv\mathsf{pullRx}_{\mathcal{B}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}}^{x}{u}

by
Definition

67

{}\equiv\mathsf{pushRx}_{\mathcal{B}}^{x}{u}

\mathsf{rx}_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}}^{x}{u}

by
Definition

28

{}\equiv\mathsf{rx}_{\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}}^{x}{u}

by
Definition

56

{}\equiv\mathsf{pushRx}_{\mathcal{B}}^{x}{u}

∎

Corollary 70
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a lax contravariant lens of reflexive graphs over a reflexive graph
\mathcal{A}
. Then we have a definitional equivalence
\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{op}}}{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{op}}}
of displayed reflexive graphs.

Proof.

By
Lemma

69
via duality (
Observation

68
).
∎

Although many other definitions can be obtained directly by duality, we will continue to specify various concepts and results for both oplax covariant and lax contravariant lenses in order to facilitate easy reference.

3.4
Definitional lenses of reflexive graphs

An (oplax covariant, lax contravariant) lens equips a family with a pushforward or pullback operator together with a witness of a unit law holding up to a directed edge. That the unit law holds only up to an edge is the meaning of the “oplax/lax” terminology. An important class of lenses are the ones in which the unit law holds up to definitional equality, so that the oplax or lax witness or the unit law can be given by the reflexivity datum of the component reflexive graph. We study these
definitional lenses
in the present section, with an eye to developing the theory of polynomials and partial products of lenses in
Section

3.4.4
.

Definition 71

(Definitional lens)
.

A
definitional covariant lens
is an oplax covariant lens
\mathcal{B}
over
\mathcal{A}
in which the following equations hold definitionally:

\displaystyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{push}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}}{u}\equiv u

\displaystyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{pushRx}_{\mathcal{B}}^{x}{u}\equiv\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{u}

By the same token, a
definitional contravariant lens
is a lax contravariant lens in which the following equations hold definitionally:

\displaystyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{pull}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x}}{u}\equiv u

\displaystyle x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{pullRx}_{\mathcal{B}}^{x}{u}\equiv\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{u}

Remark 72
.

We note that
Definition

71
must be understood “metatheoretically” or “judgmentally”, because definitional equality cannot be stated as a type in Martin-Löf type theory. One way to formalise this would be by means of a conservative extension such as two-level type theory
(
Annenkov et al., 2017
)
; we remain agnostic and treat
Definition

71
as a definitional extension of the
judgements
of Martin-Löf type theory rather than as a type.

Example 73

(Definitional lenses over discrete reflexive graphs)
.

Let
\mathcal{B}(x)
be a family of reflexive graphs indexed in
x:A
. Then we may turn
\mathcal{B}
canonically into an oplax covariant lens or a lax contravariant lens over the discrete reflexive graph
{\triangle}{A}
as follows using
transport
:

\displaystyle x,y:A;p:x=_{A}y,u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}(x)$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{push}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}u:\equiv p_{*}u

\displaystyle x,y:A;p:x=_{A}y,u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}(y)$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{pull}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}u:\equiv p^{-1}_{*}u

The total reflexive graph of the display of
\mathcal{B}

qua
covariant lens is then definitionally equal to the coproduct
\mathchoice{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\coprod_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
from
Definition

19
.

Computation 74

(Display of definitional lenses)
.

The display of definitional lenses is, naturally, more simple than that of ordinary lenses. If
\mathcal{B}
is a definitional covariant lens over
\mathcal{A}
, its display computes as follows:

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}}^{p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y}}v

\displaystyle\equiv\mathsf{push}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}{u}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}v

\displaystyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathsf{disp}^{+}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}}^{x}{u}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{u}

Likewise, if
\mathcal{B}
is a definitional contravariant lens, its display computes as follows:

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{disp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{disp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}}^{p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y}}v

\displaystyle\equiv u\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathsf{pull}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}{v}

\displaystyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathsf{disp}^{-}_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}}^{x}{u}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{u}

3.4.1
Example: definitional lenses from univalent families

Example 75

(Definitional lenses of from univalent families)
.

Any univalent family of path objects has a canonical
definitional
lens structure. In particular, given a univalent family of path objects
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle U,\mathcal{E}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
we can equip
\mathcal{E}
with the structure of a covariant and a contravariant lens over the reflexive graph image
U/\mathcal{E}
:

\displaystyle A_{0},A_{1}:U;f:A_{0}\mathrel{\approx_{U/\mathcal{E}}}A_{1};a:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{E}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A_{0}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{push}_{\mathcal{E}}^{f}{a}:\equiv fa

\displaystyle A_{0},A_{1}:U;f:A_{0}\mathrel{\approx_{U/\mathcal{E}}}A_{1};a:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{E}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{pull}_{\mathcal{E}}^{f}{a}:\equiv f^{-1}a

\displaystyle A:U;a:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{E}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{pushRx}_{\mathcal{E}}^{A}{a}:\equiv\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{E}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{a}

\displaystyle A:U;a:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{E}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{pullRx}_{\mathcal{E}}^{A}{a}:\equiv\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{E}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{a}

Example 76

(Definitional lenses from universes)
.

Any univalent family of types
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle U,E$}\mathclose{}{)}}
,
e.g
.
a universe, determines a univalent family of path objects
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle U,{\triangle}{E}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. In this case,
U/\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle{\triangle}{E}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\equiv U/E
is precisely the canonical path object structure on
U
determined by equivalences between components of
E
, and we may consider the corresponding definitional lens structures on
{\triangle}{E}
by specialising
Example

75
.

Example 77

(Definitional lenses from subuniverses)
.

Let
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle U,\mathcal{E}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
be a univalent family, and let
P\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}
be a proposition for every
A:U
. We can restrict
\mathcal{E}
to a family of path objects
\mathcal{E}_{P}
over
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A:U/\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{E}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\SavedStyle P\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
setting
\mathcal{E}_{P}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A,h$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\equiv\mathcal{E}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. Then
\mathcal{E}_{P}
can be equipped with the structure of a definitional covariant and contravariant lens over
U/\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{E}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
in the following way:

\displaystyle A,B:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A:U/\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{E}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\SavedStyle P\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}};f:A\mathrel{\approx_{U/\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{E}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}}B,x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{E}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\pi_{1}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{push}_{\mathcal{E}_{P}}^{f}{x}:\equiv fx

\displaystyle A,B:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A:U/\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{E}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\SavedStyle P\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}};f:A\mathrel{\approx_{U/\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{E}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}}B,x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{E}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\pi_{1}{B}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{pull}_{\mathcal{E}_{P}}^{f}{x}:\equiv f^{-1}{x}

\displaystyle A:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A:U/\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{E}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\SavedStyle P\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{E}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\pi_{1}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{pushRx}_{\mathcal{E}_{P}}^{A}{x}:\equiv\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{E}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\pi_{1}A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{x}

\displaystyle A:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A:U/\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{E}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\SavedStyle P\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle A$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},x:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{E}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\pi_{1}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{pullRx}_{\mathcal{E}_{P}}^{A}{x}:\equiv\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{E}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\pi_{1}A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{x}

3.4.2
Example: definitional lenses for finite ordinals

For each
n:\mathbb{N}
, let
\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}
be the following path object with vertices in the standard finite set with
n
elements:

\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle i:{\triangle}{\mathbb{N}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\SavedStyle i<n$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

The reflexive graph image of
\mathbb{N}
under
\mathcal{F}
is
not
univalent because
\mathbb{N}
is a set and thus each
m=_{\mathbb{N}}n
is a proposition whereas there is a proper set of equivalences from
\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle m$}\mathclose{}{)}}
to
\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle m$}\mathclose{}{)}}
for
m\geq 2
. Therefore, we cannot apply
Example

75
nor
Example

77
to obtain a good definitional lens structure on
\mathcal{F}
. Nonetheless, we can impose with some ingenuity a more restricted path object structure on
\mathbb{N}
over which it is not difficult to exhibit
\mathcal{F}
as a definitional lens.

Example 78
.

We may classify the
augmented simplices
by the following reflexive graph structure
\Delta_{a}
with vertices in the natural numbers and edges given by monotone equivalences between finite ordinals:

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\Delta_{a}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle m\mathrel{\approx_{\Delta_{a}}}n

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle f:\mathsf{Equiv}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle m$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle f:\mathsf{Equiv}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle m$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle f:\mathsf{Equiv}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle m$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle f:\mathsf{Equiv}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle m$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathsf{isMonotone}\,f

\displaystyle\mathsf{rx}_{\Delta_{a}}{n}

\displaystyle:\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle 1_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}},\mathsf{idnMono}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

We shall deduce that
Example

78
yields a path object, using the fact that the category of augmented simplices is
gaunt
in the sense that there is at most one monotone isomorphism between any two finite ordinals.

Lemma 79

(A path object classifying augmented simplices)
.

The reflexive graph
\Delta_{a}
of augmented simplices is univalent.

Proof.

Fixing
m:\mathbb{N}
, we must check that the fan
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle m$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\Delta_{a}}^{+}
is a proposition. Unfolding definitions, we have

\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\SavedStyle m$}\mathclose{}{\}}}_{\Delta_{a}}^{+}\equiv\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle n:\mathbb{N}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle n:\mathbb{N}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle n:\mathbb{N}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle n:\mathbb{N}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle f:\mathsf{Equiv}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle m$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle f:\mathsf{Equiv}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle m$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle f:\mathsf{Equiv}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle m$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle f:\mathsf{Equiv}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle m$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}},\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathsf{isMonotone}\,f
. As there can be at most one monotone equivalence between
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle m$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
and some
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
, it remains only to observe that
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
being equinumerous with
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle m$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
implies
m=n
.
∎

Example 80

(Definitional lens structure of finite ordinals)
.

We may exhibit definitional covariant and contravariant lens structures on
\mathcal{F}
over
\Delta_{a}
like so:

\displaystyle m,n:\mathbb{N};\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle f,f^{\leq}$}\mathclose{}{)}}:m\mathrel{\approx_{\Delta_{a}}}n,i:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle m$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{push}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle f,f^{\leq}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{i}:\equiv fi

\displaystyle m,n:\mathbb{N};\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle f,f^{\leq}$}\mathclose{}{)}}:m\mathrel{\approx_{\Delta_{a}}}n,i:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{pull}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle f,f^{\leq}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{i}:\equiv f^{-1}i

\displaystyle m:\mathbb{N};i:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle m$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{pushRx}_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}{i}:\equiv\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle m$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{i}

\displaystyle m:\mathbb{N};i:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle m$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle\vdash\mathsf{pullRx}_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}{i}:\equiv\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{F}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle m$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{i}

3.4.3
Definitional replacement of lenses

We have seen that in some examples of definitional lenses, the reflexive graph structure on the base required some ingenuity. In this section, we describe a general method to reparameterise any lens as a
definitional
lens over a different base.

Construction 81

(Flattening of lenses)
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be an oplax covariant or lax contravariant lens of reflexive graphs over a reflexive graph
\mathcal{A}
. We may equip

or

respectively called the
flattening
of
\mathcal{B}
onto
\mathcal{A}
.

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}_{{\downarrow}\mathcal{B}}^{+}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle:\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

\displaystyle x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}_{{\downarrow}\mathcal{B}}^{+}}}y

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p:x\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{A}}}y$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle p^{\prime}:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\to\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle y$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle u:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}\mathsf{push}_{\mathcal{B}}^{p}{u}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathcal{B}\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle \displaystyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}_{{\downarrow}\mathcal{B}}^{+}}{x}

\displaystyle:\equiv\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{rx}_{\mathcal{A}}{x},1_{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{B}{x}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}},\mathsf{pushRx}_{\mathcal{B}}^{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathcal{A}_{{\downarrow}\mathcal{B}}^{-}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
